## Claude · 2026-11-02 16:38

Both systems work end to end, but the smoke test shows a timing problem:

| | Result | Time | Tokens |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A · Qwen alone** | wrong: "there's no official figure…" (it has no data) | **215 s** | 4,947 |
| **B · Qwen + harness** | right: 9.5 %, [8.9 %, 10.3 %] | 39 s | 9,928 |

Without tools, Qwen with thinking on spends minutes reasoning about what it can't know. If every A run takes about 200 s, the 36 A runs alone take **about 2 hours**, and the full benchmark **about 2.5–3 hours** instead of 40 min.

**Two choices:**
1. **Keep thinking on for both (recommended).** It's the fairest comparison: same model, same settings, only the harness differs. It just takes longer. It runs in the background and resumes if stopped, so it doesn't need watching, but the laptop and Ollama must stay on for about 3 hours.
2. **Thinking off for A only.** That's how the kit's `harness.py` runs level 0 (`"think": False`), and it takes about 5 s per run, so the total is about 35 min. A teacher could object that A was handicapped. We'd have to state it in `summary.md`, and A's answers would be weaker for two reasons at once (no harness and no thinking).

Which one?

## User · 2026-11-02 16:42

ok first tell me is the thinking on for the model + harness?

## Claude · 2026-11-02 16:42

Yes. **B (Qwen + harness) runs with thinking on.** It's the app's default (`tgv` without `--no-think`), and the benchmark uses the same setting for both systems (`THINK = True` in `run_benchmark.py`).

That's why B was correct in 39 s: each of its turns is short because the tools give it real data to reason about. A, with nothing to read, keeps reasoning for 215 s.

So with option 1, the only difference between A and B is the harness. With option 2, A would also lose its thinking, which mixes two differences.

## Claude · 2026-11-02 16:43

The benchmark is running in the background: 72 runs (12 questions × 3 tries × 2 systems), with thinking on for both. It should take **about 3 hours**, mostly because Qwen alone reasons for a long time when it has no data.

**What it produces:**
- **`benchmark/results/results.csv`:** one row per run, written as soon as each run ends
- **`benchmark/results/progress.log`:** one line per run (task, system, right/wrong, seconds)
- **`benchmark/traces/system.jsonl`:** full traces of every "Qwen + harness" run, which is also the brick 1 evidence
- **`benchmark/traces/bare_model.jsonl`:** every "Qwen alone" answer, with its reasoning
- **`benchmark/results/summary.md`:** written at the end: accuracy per run, mean ± standard deviation, min–max, time and tokens, and one row per question

**While it runs:**
- **Keep the laptop on and Ollama running.** If it stops (shutdown, Ollama down), running `python benchmark/run_benchmark.py` again resumes where it left off, without redoing finished runs.
- **Avoid using the `tgv` app or Ollama heavily.** They share the GPU, which would slow both and change the timings.
- I'll be notified when it finishes. Then I'll read the results and pick a real failure to explain from its trace.

**Also done:**
- `tests/test_benchmark.py` covers truths, scoring, resume, mean and spread: 20 tests, 272 in total
- `plan.md` shows step 5 in progress
- the session log is updated

The benchmark doesn't need my attention while it runs. Do you want to start on something that doesn't use Ollama in the meantime? The README (step 7) would fit. Or wait for the results.

## Claude · 2026-11-02 17:01

I've written `README.md` and a small `Modelfile`. Nothing is committed yet. I checked the facts it states against the machine: data file about 3 MB, model 3.3 GB, Ubuntu 24.04. Here it is:

---

# TGV Delay Analyst

Ask questions about the punctuality of French high-speed trains (TGV) in plain English or French — *"Quel est le taux de retard de Paris Lyon vers Marseille en 2024 ?"* — and get an answer where **every number was computed by code** on SNCF open data.

A small local model (Qwen3.5-4B) cannot be trusted with numbers on its own. This project is the harness around it: tools that compute on the data, a system prompt with the real column names, and a guard that blocks any answer containing a number that no tool printed.

Final project, *Coding for AI & Data Science* (PGE3, 2026–2027). Bricks 1, 3, 6, 8, 9 — see EVIDENCE.md for the proof of each one.

## Model
**Qwen3.5-4B, Q4_K_M, running locally in Ollama**, with a 16K-token context (`Modelfile`). No cloud model and no API key are used: the Cerebras fallback of the project rules was not needed.

Tested on Ubuntu 24.04, Python 3.12, a laptop with an RTX 3050 Ti 4 GB GPU (Ollama splits the model between GPU and CPU). About 3.3 GB of disk for the model, 8 GB of RAM.

## Setup (fresh clone)
You need Python 3.11+ and Ollama.
```bash
git clone https://github.com/BOUADEIKHEIREDDINE/tgv-delay-analyst.git
cd tgv-delay-analyst

# 1. Python environment
python3 -m venv .venv
source .venv/bin/activate            # Windows: .venv\Scripts\activate
pip install -r requirements.txt
pip install -e .                     # creates the `tgv` command

# 2. The model (once, ~3.3 GB download)
ollama pull qwen3.5:4b-q4_K_M
ollama create qwen3.5-4b-16k -f Modelfile

# 3. The data (SNCF open data, ~3 MB, then cleaned)
python scripts/download_data.py
python scripts/clean_data.py
```
Ollama must be running (`ollama serve`, or the Ollama app / service). The default settings need nothing else; to change them see `.env.example`.

## Run
```bash
tgv                                   # full-screen app (type /help inside)
tgv "What share of TGV trains from Paris Montparnasse to Bordeaux St Jean arrived late in 2023?"
tgv --no-think "..."                  # faster, less reliable
tgv --simple                          # line-by-line mode in the normal terminal
```
`python main.py` does the same as `tgv` without `pip install -e .`. An answer takes about 30–90 s on the test laptop (thinking on). The app shows each step: the code Qwen wrote, what it printed, the guard's verdict, and where each number comes from. `/trace` shows the last question in full, `/log` the cleaning log.

Questions to try: *Quel est le taux de retard à l'arrivée de Paris Lyon vers Marseille St Charles en 2024 ?* · *How many TGV trains were cancelled in 2023, all routes together?* · *What was the cancellation rate of TGV trains from Paris Lyon to Nice Ville in 2024?*

## Tests
```bash
python -m pytest
```
272 tests, about 15 s. They need neither the model nor the downloaded data: the model is replaced by a fake that replays real Qwen replies, and the data by small files in `tests/fixtures/`.

## Benchmark (brick 9)
```bash
python benchmark/run_benchmark.py             # 12 questions × 3 runs × 2 systems (~3 h)
python benchmark/run_benchmark.py --runs 1    # one run each
python benchmark/run_benchmark.py --summary   # rebuild the table from results.csv
```
Qwen alone against Qwen + this harness, on questions whose answer is computed separately with pandas. Results: `benchmark/results/summary.md`; traces: `benchmark/traces/`.

## How it works
```
question ─► agent.py ─► Qwen (llm.py) ─► asks for a tool? ──yes──► tools.py ─► result back to Qwen ─┐
                ▲                                                                                   │
                └──────────────────────────────────── next turn ◄──────────────────────────────────┘
                          no: final answer ─► guard.py: every number printed by a tool?
                                                 yes ─► shown    no ─► one retry, then blocked
                          every step saved in traces/traces.jsonl
```
Plus a table of the main files and their role.

## What the data can't tell
- **Why** trains were late: only monthly shares of broad causes per route.
- Whether **your** train will be late: monthly totals per route.
- An **official national rate**: one train can count on several routes, so SNCF warns that route figures must not be added up (see `data/RULES.md`).
- The 95 % interval treats trains as independent; trains of the same day share the weather, strikes and works, so the real uncertainty is wider.
- The guard checks that each number was printed by a tool, not that it answers the question.

## Data
Régularité mensuelle TGV par liaisons (AQST), SNCF Open Data, licence ODbL. Downloaded by the script, not stored in the repository. No personal data.

## AI usage
Built with AI assistants, directed file by file. Conversation exports and session notes: `ai_log/`.

---

**Notes:**
- **Two numbers to update at the end:** the test count (272) and the benchmark time (~3 h), once the benchmark finishes. It's going faster than the smoke test suggested; it was at task 5 of round 1 after 18 min.
- **The setup commands still need a real test on a fresh clone.** I'd do that after committing: clone into a temporary folder and run the commands one by one. The model and the 3 GB download are already on this machine, so that step is quick.
- **llama.cpp isn't mentioned.** Only Ollama was tested, and I'd rather not claim the other works.

Want any changes, or should I go ahead with the fresh-clone test?

## Claude · 2026-11-02 17:15

It's there: **step 3 of the setup**. There's no database to install; the "data" is a CSV file, and these two commands create it:

```bash
# 3. The data (SNCF open data, ~3 MB, then cleaned)
python scripts/download_data.py      # downloads the SNCF file  → data/raw/regularite-mensuelle-tgv-aqst.csv
python scripts/clean_data.py         # cleans it                → data/clean/tgv_clean.csv (+ data/cleaning_log.csv)
```

On a fresh clone, `data/raw/` and `data/clean/` don't exist, because they're gitignored, as the spec allows (« Les données, ou le script qui les télécharge »). These two commands create both. The agent then reads `data/clean/tgv_clean.csv`, and `run_python` loads it as `df`.

If someone forgets this step, the app catches it. The startup banner shows the train "cancelled" with the exact command to run (`python scripts/download_data.py && python scripts/clean_data.py`). That case is tested in `test_ui.py`.

If you think it's too easy to miss, I can make it more visible. For example, the heading could become "3. Download and clean the data (required)", plus a line explaining that the data isn't stored in the repo. Want that?

## User · 2026-11-02 17:23

what is the Modelfile

## Claude · 2026-11-02 17:23

The `Modelfile` is a small **recipe file for Ollama**. It tells Ollama how to build a custom version of a model from a base one. Ours is 2 lines (plus comments):

```
FROM qwen3.5:4b-q4_K_M
PARAMETER num_ctx 16384
```

- **`FROM qwen3.5:4b-q4_K_M`:** start from the official Qwen3.5-4B model, Q4_K_M quantization. That's the one the spec requires.
- **`PARAMETER num_ctx 16384`:** set its context length to 16,384 tokens, meaning how much text it can "see" at once: the system prompt, the question, every tool result.

**Why we need it.** By default, Ollama gives the model a short context. With our system prompt, the column list and several tool results, Qwen would start forgetting the beginning of the conversation. The spec says it explicitly: « Fixez vous-même la longueur de contexte (… num_ctx dans Ollama) … Avec 8K à 16K tokens, 8 Go de RAM suffisent. »

**How it's used**, once, during setup:
```bash
ollama pull qwen3.5:4b-q4_K_M                    # download the official model
ollama create qwen3.5-4b-16k -f Modelfile        # build our version from the recipe
```
That creates a model named **`qwen3.5-4b-16k`**, the name `llm.py` uses by default.

**Why it's new.** Yesterday you created `qwen3.5-4b-16k` on your machine directly in the terminal, but the recipe was never saved in the project. A fresh clone couldn't recreate the same model. With the `Modelfile` in the repo, anyone can rebuild exactly the model we use. The architecture diagram even names it: *"start_model.sh or Modelfile"*.

## Claude · 2026-11-02 17:47

Oui, c'est exactement ça, et **le résultat est là : `272 passed in 14.92s`**. Sur un clone tout neuf, c'est précisément ce que demande R2 : « Ils passent sur un clone tout neuf ». ✅

**Comment lire la sortie :**
- **Chaque point `.`** = un test réussi. `tests/test_guard.py ......` veut dire que tous les tests de ce fichier sont passés.
- Un test raté s'afficherait avec un **`F`** (ou `E` pour une erreur), puis pytest montrerait en rouge le test, la ligne et la valeur attendue contre la valeur obtenue.
- **La dernière ligne** est le bilan : 272 passés, 0 raté, en 15 s.

pytest n'écrit pas de fichier de résultats : l'écran est le résultat. Si tu veux voir le **nom** de chaque test au lieu des points :
```bash
python -m pytest -v                    # une ligne par test : "test_invented_number_is_blocked PASSED"
python -m pytest tests/test_guard.py -v   # un seul fichier
```

**Pour les profs :** ce résultat ira dans `EVIDENCE.md` (étape 9), avec la commande `python -m pytest` et le bilan attendu (`272 passed`). Ils pourront le relancer eux-mêmes.

**Bilan du test sur clone neuf jusqu'ici :** installation ✅, modèle ✅, données ✅ (mêmes chiffres de nettoyage), tests ✅. Il reste deux choses :
1. **Le `tgv` qui a affiché "Goodbye 👋" :** l'application plein écran s'est-elle ouverte (train, cadre de question) avant que tu quittes, ou a-t-elle affiché "Goodbye" tout de suite ?
2. **Une vraie question** dans le clone :
   ```bash
   tgv "How many TGV trains were cancelled in 2023, all routes together?"
   ```
   La bonne réponse est **15,482**.

## Claude · 2026-11-02 17:48

If by "right banner" you mean **the panel on the right** (session, conversations, starter questions, shortcuts), it's probably not a bug: **the app hides that panel when the terminal is narrower than 100 columns** (`SIDEBAR_MIN_SCREEN = 100` in `app.py`). The panel needs 38 columns, and on a narrow window it would squeeze the conversation too much.

Your new terminal for the clone is probably smaller than the one you usually use. Two fixes:
- **Press `Ctrl+B` inside the app** to show or hide the panel at any width.
- **Or make the terminal window wider** (or fullscreen) **before** launching `tgv`. Above 100 columns the panel shows from the start.

To check the width of your terminal:
```bash
tput cols        # under 100 → the panel starts hidden
```

The panel's "History" will also be **empty** in the clone. Saved conversations live in `conversations/`, which is gitignored, so they stay on each machine.

If you meant something else (the train banner at the top looking different, or the panel missing even with `Ctrl+B`), tell me what you see, or paste a screenshot, and I'll look.

class QuestionBox(TextArea):
    """The question box: Enter sends, \\ + Enter (or Shift+Enter) adds a new line.

    Everything else (mouse selection, Shift+arrows, deleting a selection,
    Ctrl+Z) is standard TextArea behavior.
    """

    class Submitted(Message):
        """The user pressed Enter: `text` is the whole question."""

        def __init__(self, text):
            super().__init__()
            self.text = text

    async def _on_key(self, event):
        if event.key in NEW_LINE_KEYS:
            event.stop()
            event.prevent_default()
            self.insert("\n")
            return
        if event.key == "enter":
            event.stop()
            event.prevent_default()
            row, column = self.cursor_location
            if column > 0 and self.document.get_line(row)[column - 1] == ui.NEW_LINE_KEY:
                self.replace("\n", (row, column - 1), (row, column))  # the \ becomes a new line
            else:
                self.post_message(self.Submitted(self.text))
            return

    def get_selection(self, selection: Selection) -> tuple[str, str] | None:
        """Get the text under the selection.

        !!! note
            Implement this method if are building custom widget. If you just want to get the currently
            selected text, then see [`Screen.get_selected_text`](textual.screen.Screen.get_selected_text)


        Args:
            selection: Selection information.

        Returns:
            Tuple of extracted text and ending (typically "\n" or " "), or `None` if no text could be extracted.
        """
        visual = self._render()
        if isinstance(visual, (Text, Content)):
            text = str(visual)
        else:
            return None
        return selection.extract(text), "\n"

## Claude · 2026-11-02 17:56

I've pushed the copy/paste fix (commit `1f6f414`). The tests pass (276).

## Why I couldn't just "make Ctrl+V work"
It's how terminals work, not a bug in our code. Vim and htop behave the same way.
- **Paste:** a program running inside a terminal **can't read your clipboard**. Only the terminal can, and it sends the text when you press **its** paste key, **Ctrl+Shift+V**. In our app, plain Ctrl+V only pastes the app's own internal clipboard, which is empty.
- **Selecting:** the app captures the mouse for scrolling and clicks, so a normal drag doesn't select. Holding **Shift** while dragging makes the terminal select instead. Then **Ctrl+Shift+C** copies.

## What changed
- **Ctrl+V** in the question box now shows a notice, *"Paste with ctrl+shift+v (the terminal's paste)"*, instead of doing nothing.
- **The right panel's shortcuts** now include `shift+drag select · ctrl+shift+v paste`.
- **`/help`** lists both keys under the commands.
- **The README** mentions them in the "Run" section.
- **4 new tests**, including one that sends a real terminal paste and checks it lands in the question box.

## Getting the changes into your clone
```bash
cd ~/Desktop/testing_fresh_clone/tgv-delay-analyst
git pull                      # downloads and applies the new commits
source .venv/bin/activate     # if this terminal doesn't have the venv active yet
tgv
```
- **No reinstall needed:** `pip install -e .` installed the project in "editable" mode, so `tgv` uses the files in the folder directly.
- **Tests:** `python -m pytest` in the clone should now say **276 passed**.

The benchmark is still running: round 2, task 5, about 1 h 10 min in. So far the "Qwen + harness" answers are coming back correct and the "Qwen alone" answers wrong, as expected.

## Claude · 2026-11-02 18:03

You're in GNOME Terminal, where the fix should be **Shift held during the whole drag**. Two quick checks:

1. **Press and hold Shift first**, then click and drag over the answer, and only release Shift after releasing the mouse. The text should highlight in the terminal's own colour (not the app's), then **Ctrl+Shift+C** copies it.
2. **Did the text highlight at all?** If it highlighted but nothing got copied, it's only the copy key: use Ctrl+Shift+C, not Ctrl+C.

If Shift+drag really doesn't select anything, I have three ways to fix it in the app:
- **(a) A `--no-mouse` option for `tgv`:** the app stops capturing the mouse, so normal selection works everywhere. You lose mouse scrolling and clicking in the panel (the keyboard still works).
- **(b) A `/copy` command:** writes the last answer to `last_answer.txt`, which you can open and copy from. It works in every terminal.
- **(c) Make answers selectable inside the app** with a plain drag. But GNOME Terminal blocks apps from writing to your clipboard, so the copied text could only be pasted back **inside** the app, not into another program. I don't recommend it.

Can you try Shift+drag once and tell me what happens?

    def copy_to_clipboard(self, text: str) -> None:
        """Copy text to the clipboard.

        !!! note

            This does not work on macOS Terminal, but will work on most other terminals.

        Args:
            text: Text you wish to copy to the clipboard.
        """
        self._clipboard = text
        if self._driver is None:
            return
        import base64

        base64_text = base64.b64encode(text.encode("utf-8")).decode("utf-8")
        self._driver.write(f"\x1b]52;c;{base64_text}\a")

'''def to_text(renderable, width):
    """Draw any rich renderable (panel, table, group...) as styled Text, `width` columns wide.

    The full-screen app shows its history as Text because Textual can only select
    text with the mouse, not panels or tables. Colors and borders stay the same.
    """
    console = Console(width=width, force_terminal=True, color_system="truecolor",
                      highlight=False, file=io.StringIO())
    lines = console.render_lines(renderable, console.options.update_width(width), pad=False)
    text = Text(no_wrap=True, overflow="crop", end="")
    for number, line in enumerate(lines):
        for segment in line:
            if not segment.control:
                text.append(segment.text, segment.style)
        if number < len(lines) - 1:
            text.append("\\n")
    return text

**Claude:** Claude Code doesn't take over the screen, so the terminal selects and scrolls; our full-screen app must do it itself. Textual supports drag selection with auto-scroll at the edges (ENABLE_SELECT_AUTO_SCROLL) but only selects Text, and our history blocks are Rich panels/tables. Implemented: `ui.to_text(renderable, width)` draws any renderable as styled Text (same look); `app.Block(Static)` renders its content through it at the current width (cached, height from the same text) — every history block is now selectable by plain drag, with auto-scroll. Ctrl+C: QuestionBox.action_copy copies its own selection, else the history selection (screen.copy_text). TgvApp.copy_to_clipboard: Textual's OSC 52 + internal clipboard, plus the first system tool found (wl-copy, xclip, xsel, pbcopy); GNOME Terminal (VTE_VERSION set) ignores OSC 52, so without a tool a notice says "sudo apt install wl-clipboard". Help: TERMINAL_KEYS for line mode, APP_KEYS for the app; panel shortcuts "drag select · ctrl+c copy"; README updated. Tests: to_text (panel, wrapping), every block selectable, real mouse drag + Ctrl+C copies the selection, system tool used, no tool, GNOME notice. 283 passed. Committed and pushed.

## Claude · 2026-11-02 18:10

I've pushed the new selection (commit `eb67aff`). It works like Claude now: plain drag to select, and dragging to the top or bottom edge scrolls the conversation while you keep selecting. The tests pass (283).

**What changed:**
- **Every history block is selectable:** answers, tool calls, the banner. They're drawn as styled text instead of Rich panels, and they look the same. Textual (our UI library) can only select text, which is why nothing selected before.
- **Ctrl+C copies the selection**, even while the question box has the focus.
- **`/help`, the panel and the README** show the new keys: `drag select · ctrl+c copy`.
- **New tests**, including a real mouse drag over the history followed by Ctrl+C.

**One install on your machine to paste outside the app:**
```bash
sudo apt install wl-clipboard
```
Apps copy through a terminal code (OSC 52), and **GNOME Terminal ignores it**. So the app also uses `wl-copy`, the standard clipboard tool on Wayland, when it's installed. Without it, copying still works **inside** the app (Ctrl+V pastes it into the question box), and the app shows a notice with that install command. Other terminals (kitty, VS Code, Windows Terminal) work without it.

**Getting it into your clone:**
```bash
cd ~/Desktop/testing_fresh_clone/tgv-delay-analyst
git pull
source .venv/bin/activate     # if not active in that terminal
tgv
```
Then drag over an answer, press Ctrl+C, and paste anywhere with Ctrl+V (once `wl-clipboard` is installed). `python -m pytest` should say **283 passed**.

The benchmark is still running: round 2, task 8, about 1 h 25 min in.

## Claude · 2026-11-02 18:13

Copying works: what you pasted is the app's conversation, selected and copied with the new drag + Ctrl+C. And the answer is correct: **12.0 %, [11.4 %, 12.6 %], 1,385 / 11,554**, exactly benchmark question 3's truth.

Reading it closely, I see **one small bug and one thing that's expected:**

**1. Bug: wrong source for "12.0".** The answer shows `12.0[1]`, as if the number came from call 1 (`describe_dataset`). It actually comes from call 3 (`confidence_interval`, "12.0 %"). The cause: `describe_dataset` prints "month number (**1-12**)", and the source finder takes the **first** tool call that printed a matching number. The integer 12 rounded to one decimal is 12.0, so it matches. The guard verdict itself is fine; only the `[1]` label is wrong.
- **Proposed fix:** search the tool calls **from the most recent backwards**. The final answer almost always uses the latest results, so "12.0" would get `[3]`. It's a one-line change in `agent.find_sources`, plus a test reproducing this exact case.

**2. Expected: it's slow right now** (142 s for the question, 192 s for "thankyou"). The benchmark is running in the background on the same GPU, so both slow down. Normally a question takes 30–90 s. It's still worth noting that even "thank you" makes Qwen think for a long time with thinking on. `--no-think` would answer that in a few seconds.

**Also visible:** in call 2, Qwen printed whole pandas columns inside its f-strings, hence the long list of numbers. It's harmless (the answer is right and verified), just messy code from a 4B model.

Should I fix the source label (point 1)?

 ("| Check on a real fresh clone (with README, step 5) |", "| ✅ passed on the fresh clone (272 at the time) |"),
 ("7. ⏳ `README.md` (title, setup, Ollama/model, how to run) + test on a real fresh clone (R1 + R2)",
  "7. ✅ `README.md` + `Modelfile` + fresh-clone test (all steps OK). Also from the test: copy/paste and mouse selection in the app, source labels fixed"),
]:
    assert s.count(a) == 1, a[:50]; s = s.replace(a, b)
p.write_text(s)
EOF

print(f[:8], first, "->", last, "user-text msgs:", users, types)
